Maybe use a "random greedy" construction of p_i as continuous values: Start with all zeros; repeatedly add mass to a position that minimizes maximum increase in convolution? Since score scales, we can build relative masses. This is likeonline load balancing: We want final p. If we add infinitesimal mass dx at position i, change c_k = 2 p_{k-i} dx + O(dx^2). Greedy algorithm:
- Initialize p=0. For steps M (large, e.g., 100k), at each step choose position i minimizing max_k (p*p + 2 p shifted_i) or simply minimize max over active lags of p_{k-i}. Add dx=1/M. Normalize at end.
This is like water-filling. We can vectorize: For active lag set A, contribution score for adding at i = max_{k∈A} p_{k-i}. Choose i with minimal. This avoids peak positions. Complexity each step O(|A|) if efficient? M large * n maybe. But can approximate by continuous density: choose p_i proportional to exp(-λ max active shifted). Not.

Alternatively use "repulsion": each mass repels other masses at positions that sum to active lags. We can solve ODE.

  - grad_p = 2*sum_{k active} w_k * shift_f(k). Direct.
  - natural grad_s = p*(grad_p - dot(p,grad_p)). (Because derivative of objective wrt p on tangent.) Actually if objective F(p)=logsumexp(beta c)/? Implement adaptive step:
```

logfix = -eta*G - max
fix=exp; normalize
log_p = log(p) - mean?

log_p_new -= max; p=exp; normalize
```

def shift_sum(p, lags, weights, n):
    G = zeros(n)
    for k,w in zip(lags, weights):
        s=max(0,k-n+1); e=min(n-1,k)
        if s<=e:
            idx = k - np.arange(s,e+1)
            G[s:e+1] += w * p[idx]
    return G
```

Let's detail new optimizer with full logsumexp gradient.

Pseudo:
```
def _autocorr_fft(f, N): # N precomputed >=2n-1
    F = np.fft.rfft(f, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F*F, N)[:2*n-1]
    # clean tiny negatives
    return F, c

def _grad_fft(p, What, N):
    F = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    h = np.fft.irfft(F*What, N)
    L=2*n-1
    G = h[L-1 : L-n-1 : -1]
    return F, G
```
But precomputing What requires w_rev length L padded to N. If What cached, we need F for p. We can combine c and G in one function:
```
def _c_and_G(p, What, N):
    F = rfft(p,N)
    c = irfft(F*F,N)[:L]
    h = irfft(F*What,N)[:3*n-2]
    G = h[L-1:L-n-1:-1]
    return c, G
```
This uses two irffts. Could skip c if only need G? Need score. We can compute c each iter.

If N=32768 for n=6121, h length up to 18361, okay.

Weight computation:
```
def _make_weights(c, temp):
    # c length L
    cmax = c.max()
    if temp <= 0: top weights
    z = (c - cmax) / temp
    z = np.clip(z, -50, 0)
    w = np.exp(z)
    # If too many underflow all but top sum small? ok.
    s = w.sum()
    if s <= 0: w = zeros; w[argmax]=1
    else: w /= s
    return w, cmax
```
But if temp is very small, only top weights; What sparse but FFT still. If weight vector has many zeros, direct might be faster but we use FFT.

Temp/gamma schedules. Let's define optimizer parameters as dict and variants.

```
def optimize_lse(f0, n, deadline, rng, temp_start=None, temp_end=None, gamma=1.0, rho=0.9, recompute=5, max_iters=..., verbose=False, label=""):
    t = np.asarray(f0, float); t=np.maximum(t,0); t=t/t.sum()
    logp = np.log(t)  # normalized? t sums1 so log values sum exp=1
    logp -= logp.max() # but if t sums1, logsumexp=0; subtract max just for stable
    # maintain p and logp normalized: after subtract max, p=exp(logp)/sum not 1? We can store unnormalized logp and compute p each time.
```
Let's store `logp` unnormalized such that p=exp(logp-max)/sum. At initialization, set logp = log(t + eps). Then normalize by max. It doesn't matter if sum exp not 1; p normalized when used.
For blend, we need logfix normalized similarly (unnormalized with max subtracted). We can blend raw log values then normalize. But raw logp may have arbitrary offset; logfix offset max subtracted. To blend stably, we can normalize both distributions as `lp = logp - logsumexp(logp)` and `lf = logfix - logsumexp(logfix)` (so sums to 1 exactly in log). Then blended `logp = rho*lp + (1-rho)*lf`, no need normalize? Sum may not 1 due to geometric mean; normalize by logsumexp. This is clean. Computing logsumexp O(n) each step (already). We'll maintain `logp_norm` (sum exp=1) and p. At each step, after update, normalize logp_norm via logsumexp. p = exp(logp_norm) (since sum1). But if logp_norm values large negative, okay.

Implementation of normalize logp:
```
def _normalize_logp(logp):
    m = logp.max()
    logz = np.log(np.exp(logp - m).sum())
    return logp - m - logz
```
This is two exps; we already compute p = exp(logp - m); s = p.sum(); logp -= m + log(s). Could combine.

In loop:
- p = exp(logp_norm); sum should be 1 but due to numeric s close1; we can use p directly for F maybe normalize: `p /= p.sum()` to be safe.

Simpler: keep p as array; after update, p = p**rho * fix**(1-rho); p = np.maximum(p, eps); p /= p.sum(); logp = np.log(p). This avoids logsumexp complexity. But p**rho with p small maybe underflow; use log if needed. Values ~1e-4, p**0.9 ~1e-3, fine. If p approaches 0, p^rho 0; may kill support. We can add floor. Use log for stability:
   `logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)`
   `logfix = np.log(fix + 1e-300)`
   `lognew = rho*logp + (1-rho)*logfix`
   `lognew -= lognew.max(); p = exp(lognew); p /= p.sum()`

```
Gspan = G.max()-G.min()
if Gspan < 1e-30: delta=0
else:

   logfix = -eta*(G - G.mean())? If subtract mean, weights sum? G positive. Using -eta*G: fix normalized; offset irrelevant. Variation = eta*Gspan.
   logfix -= logfix.max(); fix=exp; fix/=sum
```

- progress = it / expected_iters (if max_iters known) but we don't know due deadline. Use time-based? Simpler use iteration count decaying:
```
gamma = gamma0 + (gamma_max - gamma0)*(1 - exp(-it/tau))
```
Start small, grow.

Since delta has two nonzero, conv(p,delta) can be computed as p shifted by i and j times deltas. Specifically conv(p,delta)_k = delta_i p_{k-i} + delta_j p_{k-j}, with valid indices. ```
def _c_G_fft(p, What, N, n):
    L=2*n-1
    F = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F*F, N)[:L]
    if What is not None:
        h = np.fft.irfft(F*What, N)[:3*n-2]
        G = h[L-1:L-n-1:-1]
        return F, c, G
    else: return F, c, None
```

Perturb modes:
```
def perturb_p(p, rng, strength, n):
    r = rng.integers(0,5)
    logp = np.log(p+1e-300)
    if r==0:
       noise = rng.normal(size=n)
       noise -= noise.mean()
       logp += strength*noise
    elif r==1:
       a = rng.normal(size=n); a = a - a[::-1]; logp += strength*0.5*a
    elif r==2: exponential tilt
    elif r==3: sinusoidal asymmetric
    else: random low-pass noise (size blocks)
    logp -= max; p=exp; normalize; floor
```

Let's design to minimize convolution. Suppose we concatenate two blocks A and B. Convolution = A*A + B*B + cross. If A and B separated in index, cross lags distinct from self. We can choose amplitudes to balance peaks. This is like building a probability distribution as a mixture of measures on intervals. Even if continuous optimum poor, recursive blocking can exploit discrete no-carry for certain scales. For example, split length n into two halves. Then convolution = α^2 (n/2 scaled) self A on lags 0..n-1, (1-α)^2 self B on lags n..2n-2, cross α(1-α)(A*B) on middle lags. If we choose α small/large, peaks from self and cross can balance. What about using `scipy.optimize` for small low-dimensional param families to find excellent starts? We can implement a param family with 5 chirp/blocks and optimize via Nelder-Mead (scipy). This could give good basin.

Param family: `p_i = exp(∑_{r=1}^m w_r cos(λ_r i + φ_r))` positive. Conv via FFT, score gradient can be approximated finite or analytic. We can use scipy.optimize.minimize (bounded) with Nelder-Mead (derivative-free) for 2m dims. Dimension maybe 6-10. Since one evaluation cost FFT O(n log n) ~0.1ms, can do thousands evals. We can choose λ integer modes. This is like optimizing log p as a trigonometric polynomial with M modes. It can capture chiral asymmetric patterns. Very promising and low-dimensional! We can optimize full logp with only M modes (e.g., 20) via scipy, then LSE for full.

Why not just use full LSE? Low-dim optimization can find global-ish via multiple random initializations and exploit scipy's Nelder-Mead, providing good starts. Also can directly target score with exact max (nonsmooth) but Nelder-Mead handles.

Potential issue: The objective score can increase during updates. We track best, but optimizer may diverge. ```

elif stall > patience:
   # if current much worse, revert
   if score > best_local * 1.02:
       p= best_p.copy()
   p = perturb(p, strength=0.05+0.2*prog, rng)
   recompute weights
   stall=0
```

Now final polish. Let's implement a simple pair coordinate polish using full FFT candidate evaluation but limited moves, to squeeze last bits. Since LSE likely near local optimum, pair moves can help.

`polish_pairs(p, n, N, deadline, rng)`:
- p normalized, best score.
- Precompute c full for score? Function to evaluate candidate by full c from FFT (simple) or incremental. Full FFT per candidate maybe too slow if many. Use incremental with direct update for candidate but need max; O(n) no FFT. Let's implement incremental pair candidate efficiently.

Detailed incremental pair eval:
Inputs: p, c (current conv length L), pair i,j, total m=p_i+p_j, test x (new p_i), y=m-x, with x,y>=0. dx=x-p_i, dy=y-p_j = -dx.
We need c_try max. Rather than copy c each candidate, we can compute c_try = c + arr where arr computed. For a candidate, arr dense = 2*dx*(shift_i - shift_j) + sparse B. We can allocate `delta = np.empty(L)`? Copy c to delta and add. O(n).
```
def _score_pair_move(c, p, n, i, j, x):
    L=2*n-1
    m = p[i]+p[j]
    y = m - x
    if y < -1e-12 or x< -1e-12: return inf
    dx = x - p[i]
    if abs(dx) < 1e-14: return current_score
    # reset? We'll compute arr in a new array maybe c.copy(); but for multiple x for same pair, base c same.
    # arr dense: 2*dx*(shift_i - shift_j)
    # shift_i vector length L: for k=i..i+n-1, p[k-i]
    # We can add to candidate array.
```
To avoid copying c for each x from scratch, for a pair we can precompute `A = 2*(shift_i - shift_j)` (length L) and sparse B positions. Then for a candidate dx, c_try = c + 2*A*dx + B*dx^2. We can copy c and add. But note A already 2*(shift_i - shift_j)? Let's define A=shift_i - shift_j (length L). Then linear term = 2*dx*A. B sparse: if 2i: dx^2; if 2j: dx^2; if i+j: -2dx^2 (if i≠j; if i=j not pair). If i=j, pair move none. We'll choose i<j.
A can be computed by initializing zeros L and adding p slice at i, subtract at j. O(n). For each candidate, `c_try = c + 2*dx*A + np.zeros(L) with B terms`. Instead of allocating zeros each candidate, create c_copy = c.copy(); add 2*dx*A via vectorized; then modify B positions. This is O(L) per candidate. For 4 candidates per pair, 4L.
We can choose pairs and fractions intelligently.

How to select pairs:
- Use current c top active lags (top 5). For each active k, contribution to c_k from pairs (i,k-i). We can find the pair with largest product among i and lowest product? To reduce max lag, transfer mass from high product pair to low product pair. But pair moves i,j not necessarily within same k. Previous used total contribution across top3. We can compute `contrib[j]` for top lags as sum f_j * f_{k-j} over k top. Donor = argmax contrib, receiver = argmin contrib. This is O(K*n). Then test moving mass from donor to receiver. This targets active peaks. But to explore, also random pairs.
- For donor->receiver, total m fixed? If we only change donor/receiver, sum conserved. Try fractions of donor mass transferred to receiver: new donor = old*(1-frac), receiver += old*frac. This is x = p_i*(1-frac) if i=donor, j=receiver. Also try opposite direction? Only if receiver gets mass. Also try extreme x=0,m.
- Use line search over x in [max(0, m*(1-0.9)), min(m, p_i+0.9p_j)]? For pair move, we can try several x values relative to p_i: `p_i + delta` where delta = frac*p_i (positive/negative) and maybe make equal m/2. Since we want reduce peaks, not necessarily equal.
- For each candidate, if score improves, accept and update p,c. Then continue.

Incremental c update after accepting a move: We have p new, c new from candidate. We can set c = c_try (already computed) to avoid recompute. Need ensure c_try nonnegative clipped.

This polish can be efficient: each candidate O(n) vector ops. If we do 10k accepted/candidates, 10k*6000=60M, okay maybe seconds. We should implement in Python loops carefully.

Selecting pairs using contrib O(K*n) each outer pass; if 1000 passes, 1000*4*6000=24M, okay.

Algorithm sketch:
```
def polish_pairs(p, n, N, deadline, rng, passes=20000):
    L=2*n-1
    p=p.copy(); p/=p.sum()
    c = _autocorr_fft(p,N)[:L]
    best = 2*n*c.max()
    for pass in range(passes):
       if time>deadline: break
       # top K lags
       top = np.argpartition(c, -5)[-5:]
       top_sorted = ...
       contrib = np.zeros(n)
       for k in top:
           s=max(0,k-n+1); e=min(n-1,k)
           # contrib[j] += p[j] * p[k-j]
           idx = k - np.arange(s,e+1)? Use slice? Need product of p[s:e+1] and p[idx]. We can use slice for second: p[start:stop:-1]
           start = k-s; stop = (e-? as before)
           contrib[s:e+1] += p[s:e+1] * p[start:stop:-1]
       # donor argmax, receiver argmin excluding same
       i = argmax(contrib); j = argmin(contrib)
       if i==j: choose next min
       # also random pair with prob 0.2
       if rng.random()<0.2: i=random; j=random
       # try x values for pair (i,j)
       m = p[i]+p[j]
       # candidate x = p_i, p_i*0.8, p_i*0.5, p_i*0.2, m*0.2, m*0.8, maybe p_i + 0.5*p_j, abs?
       candidates = [0.0, m/2, p[i]*0.2, p[i]*0.5, p[i]*0.8, p[i]+0.2*p[j], p[i]+0.5*p[j], p[i]+0.8*p[j]]
       only within [0,m], not too close current.
       # precompute A if evaluating several x for same pair:
       A = np.zeros(L)
       A[i:i+n] += p[:n]
       A[i+n? wait L=2n-1, i+n-1 max; slice length n: A[i:i+n] valid if i+n <= L? i max n-1 => i+n =2n-1=L, okay.
       A[j:j+n] -= p[:n]
       # B positions: pos_ii=2i, pos_ij=i+j, pos_jj=2j.
       best_candidate = None
       for x in candidates:
          dx=x-p[i]
          if abs(dx)<1e-12: continue
          c_try = c.copy(); c_try += 2*dx*A
          # B: dx^2 at 2i and 2j, -2dx^2 at i+j
          c_try[2*i] += dx*dx
          c_try[2*j] += dx*dx
          if i != j: c_try[i+j] -= 2*dx*dx
          # if i==j, handle? skip.
          sc = 2*n*c_try.max()
          if sc < best - 1e-12: accept
       # Re-order i/j? dx sign covers x range.
       if accepted:
          p[i]=x; p[j]=m-x; p normalized? sum unchanged if x+y=m, but due round; p=p.maximum(0); p/=p.sum()
          c = c_try clipped; best=sc
       # optional if no improvement, random perturbation?
```
Need ensure A formula correct: A_k=∑? shift_i(k)=p_{k-i} valid for k=i..i+n-1. `A[i:i+n] += p`. shift_j subtract. Then c_new = c + 2dx*A + B. Check with old p_i,p_j: If dx=0 c_new=c. If move all mass from i to j (x=0, dx=-p_i, y=p_j+p_i): Should conv equal p with i zero, j increased. Formula should. For i=0,j=1 length2 p=[a,b], c=[a^2,2ab,b^2]. Move x=0, dx=-a, m=a+b, y=m. A: shift0 - shift1. L=3. A[0]=p0=a, A[1]=p1-p0=b-a, A[2]= -? shift0 valid k=0,1: p0,p1; shift1 valid k=1,2: p0,p1. A=[a, b-a, -p1=-b]. dx=-a. 2dx*A = [-2a^2, -2a(b-a), 2ab]. B: 2i=0: a^2; 2j=2: a^2; i+j=1: -2a^2. c_new: c0 a^2-2a^2+a^2=0; c1 2ab-2ab+2a^2-2a^2? Wait -2a(b-a)= -2ab+2a^2; plus -2a^2 = -2ab? c1=0? But expected c1=0 if p=[0,a+b]? yes; c2 b^2+2ab+a^2 = (a+b)^2 good. Works.

We should also allow pair moves that change total? Since score scale invariant, sum conservation not required; but moving mass with renorm globally might help. Pair sum conservation enough local.

Now, integrating all. Let's outline code structure:

Top-level imports: `import numpy as np, time, math` maybe `from scipy.optimize import minimize` if using trig? We can omit scipy to reduce risk. The allowed but not necessary. Use only numpy/time.

Globals: no lambdas.

Functions:
1. `def _next_pow2(m): return 1 << (int(m)-1).bit_length()` (handle m<=1).
2. `def _normalize(p): p=np.maximum(p,0.0); s=p.sum(); if s<1e-300: return np.ones(len(p))/len(p); return p/s`
3. `def _score_from_c(c,n): cmax=float(np.max(c)); return 2.0*n*cmax` (assuming sum1).
4. `def _autocorr(p, N): F=rfft; c=irfft; c=max(c,0); return c` maybe no F.
   For optimizer, use `_cG_fft`.
5. `def _cG_fft(p, What, N, n): ...` returns c, G. If What None? For direct not use. We'll always have What for FFT mode. Need L.
   ```
   F=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
   c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)[:2*n-1]
   c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
   h=np.fft.irfft(F*What,N)
   L=2*n-1
   G=np.asarray(h[L-1:L-n-1:-1], dtype=np.float64)

6. `def _shift_sum_direct(p, lags, weights, n):`
   ```
   G=np.zeros(n)
   for t in range(len(lags)):
      k=int(lags[t]); w=weights[t]
      s=max(0,k-n+1); e=min(n-1,k)
      if s<=e:
         start=k-s
         stop=e-1 if e>0 else None
         # p[start:stop:-1] length e-s+1
         G[s:e+1] += w * p[start:stop:-1]
   return G
   ```
   Test with n=3,k=0: s=0,e=0,start=0,stop=-? e=0 => None, p[0::-1]=[p0], G0+=w p0 okay. k=1,s=0,e=1,start=1,stop=0, p[1:0:-1]=[p1,p0], G[0:2]+=w*[p1,p0] includes G0=p1,G1=p0 correct. k=2,n=3,s=0? max(0,2-3+1=0), e=2, start=2, e>0 stop=1, p[2:1:-1]=[p2,p1] for k=2: i=0 p2, i=1 p1, but i=2 p0? Wait k=2, valid j=0..2, idx=k-j: [2,1,0]. Our slice start=k-s=2, stop=e-1=1 gives p[2:1:-1] = indices 2,1 only missing 0! Because e=2, k-e=0; need include index 0. Our stop rule should be `stop = (k - e - 1) if k > e else None`? Let's derive generally: start=k-s (max idx), end=k-e (min idx). For negative step, slice `p[start : stop : -1]` includes stop+1. So to include end, stop = end-1 if end>0 else None. Here end=k-e=0, so None, not e-1. I used e-1 incorrectly. Need `min_idx = k - e`. stop = min_idx - 1 if min_idx > 0 else None.
   For k=2,e=2, min_idx=0 -> None, p[2::-1]=[2,1,0] correct.
   For k=1,e=1, min_idx=0 -> None? start=1, p[1::-1]=[1,0] correct. But earlier we thought stop=0 for k=2,e=1. Check k=2,e=1 (n>3): valid j=0,1, idx=2,1, min_idx=1, stop=0, p[2:0:-1]=[2,1] correct. So use min_idx.
   Code: `min_idx = k - e; stop = min_idx - 1 if min_idx > 0 else None`.
7. `def _make_weights(c, temp):`
   ```
   cmax=c.max()
   if temp <= 0 or not finite: top
   z=(c-cmax)/temp
   z=np.clip(z, -50.0, 0.0)
   w=np.exp(z)
   s=w.sum()
   if s <= 1e-300:
      w=np.zeros_like(c); w[np.argmax(c)]=1.0
   else: w/=s
   nz=int(np.count_nonzero(w > 1e-15))
   return w, nz, cmax
   ```
   If temp very small, only top exp ~1. Good.
8. `def _make_What(w, N):`
   `w_rev = w[w.size-1::-1].astype(np.float64, copy=False); return np.fft.rfft(w_rev, N)`
9. `def _perturb_logp(p, rng, strength, n):`
   Return normalized p.
   Use logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)
   mode = rng.integers(0,6)
   if 0: noise normal, zero mean, maybe smooth? `noise = rng.normal(size=n); noise -= noise.mean(); logp += strength*noise`
   1: antisymmetric Gaussian: a = rng.normal(size=n); a -= a.mean(); a = a - a[::-1]; logp += strength*0.5*a
   2: tilt: t=np.linspace(0,1,n); alpha=rng.uniform(-1,1); logp += strength*alpha*(t-0.5)
   3: asymmetric sine: freq=rng.integers(1, max(2,n//4)); phase; logp += strength*0.5*np.sin(2π freq t + phase)
   4: two-sided chirp? logp += strength*0.5*np.cos(π*(t-0.5)**2*freq?) maybe.
   5: block noise: divide into chunks random, smoothed.
   Normalize: `logp -= logp.max(); p=np.exp(logp); p=np.maximum(p,1e-300); p/=p.sum(); return p`
   Ensure strength not too large to overflow; logp range maybe small.
10. `def optimize_lse(f0, N, deadline, rng, ...)`:
   We'll pass n=N? Need n separately.
   Implementation details below.
11. `def polish_pairs(f, n, N, deadline, rng, verbose=False):` as above.
12. `def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs)`.

Now implement `optimize_lse` carefully.

```
def optimize_lse(f0, n, N, deadline, rng, temp_ratio0=1.0, temp_ratio1=0.02, gamma0=0.5, gamma1=3.0, rho=0.9, recompute=5, direct_threshold=64, verbose=False, label=""):
    t0=time.monotonic()
    p=np.asarray(f0, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    p=np.maximum(p,0.0); s=p.sum()
    if s<1e-300: p=np.ones(n); s=1.0
    else: p/=s
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=float('inf')
    c=np.maximum(_autocorr(p,N),0.0); best_score=2*n*c.max()
    if verbose: print(f"  {label} start {best_score:.7f}")
    # initial weights
    total_time=max(1.0, deadline-t0)
    # estimate max_iters
    max_iters = max(1000, int(total_time * 600)) # conservative
    # but N large maybe 300/s; we use deadline check.
    it=0
    ratio = temp_ratio0
    gamma = gamma0
    # compute c/weights
    cmax=best_score/(2*n)
    temp=cmax*temp_ratio0
    w, nz, cmax = _make_weights(c, temp)
    use_fft = nz > direct_threshold
    What = _make_What(w,N) if use_fft else None
    p_floor = 1e-300
    stall=0
    patience=2000
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1
        prog = min(1.0, it / max_iters)
        # update schedules maybe based on prog
        ratio = temp_ratio0 * (temp_ratio1/temp_ratio0) ** prog if temp_ratio0>0 else temp_ratio1
        gamma = gamma0 + (gamma1-gamma0) * (1.0 - math.exp(-prog*2.0)) # or prog
        # recompute weights every recompute or if None
        if use_fft:
            c, G = _cG_fft(p, What, N, n)
            cmax = float(c.max())
            score = 2.0*n*cmax
        else:
            # need c first, then G direct
            c = _autocorr(p,N)
            cmax=float(c.max()); score=2*n*cmax
        if it % recompute == 0:
            temp = cmax * ratio
            w, nz, cmax = _make_weights(c, temp)
            if nz > direct_threshold:
                What = _make_What(w,N)
                use_fft = True
                # Need G with new What; compute using F? We may already computed c without F. Compute G via FFT or direct? For simplicity, if switch to fft, compute G direct now and next iters use fft.
                G = _shift_sum_direct(p, np.where(w>1e-15)[0], w[...], n) # but nz large direct costly threshold just exceeded maybe 65 okay
            else:
                lags = np.where(w > 1e-15)[0]
               _w = w[lags]
                What = None
                G = _shift_sum_direct(p, lags, _w, n)
        else:
            if not use_fft:
                # need lags stored. Keep global lags/weights
                G = _shift_sum_direct(p, last_lags, last_weights, n)
    ...
```
Need store `last_lags,last_weights` when direct. If use_fft, G computed with cached What. When recomputing and switching, set last_lags.
To avoid direct huge when switching to FFT, we can compute G via direct with lags if nz maybe <= direct_threshold+something; or simply recompute G using a fresh `_cG_fft`? But that requires F. We can restructure: At loop start, compute F and c always, and if weights due, update What, then compute G using F and current weights (FFT if use_fft, direct if not). This avoids missing F.
Let's implement unified:
```
# maintain weight state: w, nz, What, lags, w_lags, use_fft
while:
   F = rfft(p,N)
   c = irfft(F*F,N)[:L]; clip
   score...
   if due recompute:
       temp=cmax*ratio
       w,nz,_=make_weights
       Wstate...
       if use_fft: What=make_What
       else: lags=...
   # compute G
   if use_fft:
       h=irfft(F*What,N); G=...
   else:
       G=shift_direct(p,lags,w_lags)
   # update
```
This does rfft and irfft c every iter. If direct, no G irfft. If use_fft, G irfft. Good.
Need `F` variable; use `np.fft.rfft` each iter. We can compute c and G in helper but with F reuse.

Recompute cadence: If recompute=1, weights current. If direct, lags current. If use_fft, What current, G irfft each iter.

Schedule `ratio` and `gamma` using prog. Need `max_iters` estimate. If optimization runs until deadline, prog at end 1. If `max_iters` overestimate (slower), prog smaller (less anneal) but still updates. We can choose `expected_rate = 400` for N 32768. For N=65536 150. We can set `expected_rate = 2000 if N<=16384 else (800 if N<=32768 else 300)`. max_iters=int(total*rate). But direct mode faster. Use 500. Not critical.

Alternative use time-based progress: compute `elapsed = time.monotonic()-t0; prog=elapsed/total_time`. Time call each iter overhead small? `time.monotonic()` ~50ns, fine. We'll use time-based for accurate schedule. Already check deadline. So:
```
elapsed = time.monotonic() - t0
prog = min(1.0, elapsed / total_time)
```
This requires time call each iter; okay.

Update p:
```
Gspan = G.max() - G.min()
if Gspan > 1e-300:
    eta = gamma / Gspan
    logfix = -eta * G
    logfix -= logfix.max()
    fix = np.exp(logfix)
    fs = fix.sum()
    if fs > 1e-300:
        fix /= fs
        logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)
        logf = np.log(fix + 1e-300)
        lognew = rho*logp + (1.0-rho)*logf
        lognew -= lognew.max()
        p = np.exp(lognew)
        p = np.maximum(p, 1e-300)
        s = p.sum()
        if s > 1e-300: p /= s
        else: p = best_p.copy()
```
If Gspan small, G uniform (e.g., p uniform and weights uniform). Need escape: apply random perturbation. We can if Gspan small: p = perturb(p, strength=0.1, rng); continue (maybe force weight recompute next). For uniform start with logsumexp weights triangular, Gspan not zero. Good.

Score tracking and stall:

if score < best_score - 1e-12:
    best_score=score; best_p=p.copy(); stall=0
    if verbose and it%10000==0: print
else:
    stall +=1
if stall > patience:
    # maybe revert if bad
    if score > best_score * 1.01:
       p = best_p.copy()
       # recompute c? will next loop
    strength = 0.05 + 0.3*(1.0-prog)

    force_weight = True (or set `it = recompute-1`?)
    stall=0
```

At end, return best_p, best_score.

Now, pair polish details implementation robust.

```
def polish_pairs(f, n, N, deadline, rng, max_passes=200000, verbose=False, label=""):
    L=2*n-1
    p=normalize(f); c=_autocorr(p,N); best=2*n*c.max(); best_p=p.copy()
    no_imp=0
    while time < deadline:
       # select active top 5
       if c.shape: top=argpartition...
       # contrib
      contrib=zeros(n)
       for k in top:
          s=max(0,k-n+1); e=min(n-1,k)
          if s<=e:
             start=k-s; min_idx=k-e; stop=min_idx-1 if min_idx>0 else None
             contrib[s:e+1] += p[s:e+1] * p[start:stop:-1]
       i_d=argmax(contrib); j_r=argmin(contrib)
       if i_d==j_r: choose argmax then argmin excluding
       if rng.random()<0.25:
          i_d=int(rng.integers(0,n)); j_r=int(rng.integers(0,n)); if same skip
       # Try pair (i_d,j_r) and maybe (argmax2, argmin2)
       for (i,j) in candidates:
          if i==j: continue
          m=p[i]+p[j]
          if m < 1e-15: continue
          # Build A if at least one candidate
          A=np.zeros(L)
          A[i:i+n] += p
          A[j:j+n] -= p
          # candidate x values
          # current p_i
          xs = [0.0, m/2.0, m*0.1, m*0.9, p[i]*0.1, p[i]*0.5, p[i]*0.9, p[i] + 0.5*p[j], p[i] - 0.5*p[j]]
          # filter unique within [0,m]
          for x in xs:
             if x <0 or x>m: continue
             dx=x-p[i]
             if abs(dx) < 1e-12: continue
             c_try = c.copy()
             c_try += (2.0*dx)*A
             dx2=dx*dx
             c_try[2*i] += dx2
             c_try[2*j] += dx2
             if i != j: c_try[i+j] -= 2.0*dx2
             # clip tiny negatives
             # max may be tiny negative? use max
             sc = 2.0*n*float(np.max(c_try))
             if sc < best - 1e-12:
                 p[i]=x; p[j]=m-x
                 p = np.maximum(p,0.0); p/=p.sum()
                 c = np.maximum(c_try,0.0)
                 best=sc; best_p=p.copy(); no_imp=0; improved=True; break
          if improved: break
       if not improved:
          no_imp +=1
          if no_imp > 500:
             p = _perturb_logp(p, rng, 0.05, n)
             c = _autocorr(p,N); bestP? if score worse? set best maybe unchanged? We should not reset best.
             no_imp=0
```
Need `Improve` variable reset per pass. To save time, don't build A for candidates if no x. But fine.
Potential issue: c_try may have negative values due to move making p invalid? x,y nonneg, p nonneg, c nonneg mathematically; numeric okay. Clip for max? If negative, max unaffected unless all negative impossible. Use `np.max(c_try)`.
The pair move preserves sum exactly if p normalized; after p[i]=x,p[j]=m-x, sum same. But due prior p sum maybe 1, okay. Normalize anyway.
Time: c.copy per candidate. We can accept first improving candidate, not evaluate all if found. Candidate order: try more extreme moves first? For donor->receiver, if i=donor, x smaller. We can sort candidates by distance from current descending to get big improvements first. Or random. We'll generate a set and maybe limit to 5.

Let's check the all-lag gradient with weights normalized sum1: G magnitude ~average p ~1/n. span maybe small. gamma/span gives ratio. If span very small (G nearly constant), eta huge but variation gamma. If span underflows, skip. Use `if span > 1e-30`.

One subtlety: The fixed-point `fix = exp(-eta*G)` minimizes linear approximation of weighted sum, but if G has large variation, fix may be very sparse; blend rho=0.9 keeps stable. If rho too high, progress slow; if too low, unstable. We can adjust rho schedule: early rho=0.8, late 0.95. Our params.

Could use `rho = min(0.99, rho0 + (0.99-rho0)*prog)` to stabilize late. Add to optimizer: effective_rho = rho0 + (0.98-rho0)*prog? But params include rho. Let's set `rho_eff = min(0.98, rho + 0.1*prog)` maybe. For variant with rho=0.8, late 0.9. Good.

Similarly gamma schedule maybe from gamma0 to gamma1 but if late, smaller gamma to polish. Actually to reach optimum, small gamma late. Our schedule increases gamma, maybe not. Let's reconsider. At beginning, broad weights, need strong moves to break; late, focus peaks, small moves. So gamma should start moderate, maybe increase then decrease? We can use `gamma = gamma0*(1+prog) + ...`? def _extreme_pair(contrib, n, rng, random_prob=0.2):
   if rng.random()<random_prob: i=rng.integers(0,n); j=rng.integers(0,n); return (i,j) if i!=j else None
   # get top 2 max and bottom 2 min
   order=np.argsort(contrib) # O(n log n) expensive each pass. Instead use argmax and then mask? We can use np.argpartition(contrib, 2) for top/bottom.
   top2=np.argpartition(contrib, -2)[-2:]
   bot2=np.argpartition(contrib, 2)[:2]
   # choose pair (max1,min1), maybe max2,min2 if same
   pairs=[]
   for i in top2:
      for j in bot2:
         if i!=j: pairs.append((i,j))
   return pairs
```

```

Let's pseudo-code functions fully.

```
import numpy as np
import time
import math

def _next_pow2(m):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()

def _normalize(p, min_val=1e-12):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = p.sum()
    n = p.size
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    p = p / s
    minp = min_val / n if min_val is not None else 0.0
    if minp > 0:
        p = np.maximum(p, minp)
        p /= p.sum()
    return p
```
But for evaluator, minp floor might make sum slightly. Fine. For fast score, floor introduces tiny mass. Good.

`_autocorr(p,N)`:
```
def _autocorr(p, N):
    F = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    L = 2*p.size - 1
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, N)[:L]
    return np.maximum(c, 0.0)
```

`_score_fast(p,n)`:
```
def _score_fast(p,n,N):
    c = _autocorr(p,N)
    return 2.0*n*float(c.max())
```
Use for starts. For N large, okay.

`_c_and_G_fft(p, What, N, n)` as above.
Potential issue: `F*What` creates complex array; okay.

`_shift_sum_direct` as above.

`_make_weights`:
```
def _make_weights(c, temp):
    L = c.size
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if temp <= 0 or not np.isfinite(temp):
        w=np.zeros(L); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0; return w,1,cmax
    z = (c - cmax) / temp
    z = np.minimum(z, 0.0)
    z = np.maximum(z, -50.0)
    w = np.exp(z)
    s = w.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros(L); w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0; return w,1,cmax
    w /= s
    nz = int(np.count_nonzero(w > 1e-15))
    return w, nz, cmax
```

`_make_What(w,N)`:
```
w_rev = np.ascontiguousarray(w[w.size-1::-1])
return np.fft.rfft(w_rev, N)
```

`_perturb_logp(p, rng, strength, min_val=None)`:
```
def _perturb_logp(p, rng, strength, min_val=1e-12):
    n=p.size
    logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)
    st = float(strength)
    mode = int(rng.integers(0, 6))
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    if mode == 0:
        noise = rng.normal(size=n)
        noise -= noise.mean()
        logp += st*noise
    elif mode == 1:
        a = rng.normal(size=n)
        a -= a.mean()
        a = a - a[::-1]
        logp += 0.5*st*a
    elif mode == 2:
        alpha = rng.uniform(-1.0, 1.0)
        logp += st*alpha*(t - 0.5)
    elif mode == 3:
        freq = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n//4)))
        phase = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0*np.pi)
        logp += 0.5*st*np.sin(2.0*np.pi*freq*t + phase)
    elif mode == 4:
        # antisymmetric sine
        freq = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n//4)))
        phase = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0*np.pi)
        s = np.sin(2.0*np.pi*freq*t + phase)
        s = s - s[::-1]
        logp += 0.5*st*s
    else:
        # block random smoothed
        block = max(1, n // 32)
        nb = (n + block -1)//block
        vals = rng.normal(size=nb)
        noise = np.repeat(vals, block)[:n]
        # simple circular smooth? 
        noise -= noise.mean()
        logp += 0.5*st*noise
    logp -= logp.max()
    p2 = np.exp(logp)
    p2 = np.maximum(p2, min_val/n)
    s=p2.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300: return p.copy()
    return p2/s
```
Maybe `s = s - s[::-1]` in mode4 not antisymmetric? If s is any array, s - reversed is antisymmetric. Good.

Now `optimize_lse` full:
```
def optimize_lse(f0, n, N, deadline, rng, temp_ratio0=1.0, temp_ratio1=0.02, gamma0=1.0, gamma1=0.4, rho0=0.85, rho1=0.97, recompute=5, direct_threshold=64, verbose=False, label=""):
    p=_normalize(f0, 1e-12/n?)
    c=_autocorr(p,N)
    best_score=2*n*c.max(); best_p=p.copy()
    total=max(1.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
    last_recomp = -10**9
    # weights
    cmax=best_score/(2*n)
    w,nz,cmax=_make_weights(c, cmax*temp_ratio0)
    use_fft = nz > direct_threshold
    What = _make_What(w,N) if use_fft else None
    lags = np.where(w > 1e-15)[0]
    weights = w[lags]
    iter_count=0
    stall=0
    patience=1500
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        iter_count +=1
        elapsed=time.monotonic()-t0
        prog=min(1.0, elapsed/total)
        ratio = temp_ratio0 * (temp_ratio1 / temp_ratio0) ** prog if temp_ratio0>0 else temp_ratio1
        gamma = gamma0 + (gamma1-gamma0)*prog
        rho = min(0.99, rho0 + (rho1-rho0)*prog)
        # FFT for c
        F = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
        L=2*n-1
        c = np.fft.irfft(F*F, N)[:L]
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
        cmax = float(c.max())
        score = 2.0*n*cmax
        if score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score=score; best_p=p.copy(); stall=0
            if verbose and iter_count % 10000==0: print(...)
        else:
            stall += 1
        if iter_count - last_recomp >= recompute or last_recomp < 0:
            temp = cmax * ratio
            w, nz, cmax = _make_weights(c, temp)
            if nz > direct_threshold:
                What = _make_What(w,N)
                use_fft=True
            else:
                lags = np.where(w > 1e-15)[0]
                weights = w[lags]
                use_fft=False
            last_recomp=iter_count
        if use_fft:
            h = np.fft.irfft(F * What, N)
            G = np.asarray(h[L-1:L-n-1:-1], dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            G = _shift_sum_direct(p, lags, weights, n)
        Gspan = G.max() - G.min()
        if Gspan > 1e-30:
            eta = gamma / Gspan
            logfix = -eta * G
            logfix -= logfix.max()
            fix = np.exp(logfix)
            fs = fix.sum()
            if fs > 1e-300:
                fix /= fs
                logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)
                logf = np.log(fix + 1e-300)
                lognew = rho * logp + (1.0 - rho) * logf
                lognew -= lognew.max()
                p = np.exp(lognew)
                minp = 1e-12 / n
                p = np.maximum(p, minp)
                s = p.sum()
                if s > 1e-300:
                    p /= s
                else:
                    p = best_p.copy()
        else:
            # G flat, perturb
            p = _perturb_logp(p, rng, 0.1, 1e-12)
        if stall > patience:
            if score > best_score * 1.02:
                p = best_p.copy()
            strength = 0.05 + 0.25 * (1.0 - prog)
            p = _perturb_logp(p, rng, strength, 1e-12)
            last_recomp = -10**9
            stall = 0
    # final evaluate
    c=_autocorr(p,N); score=...
    if score<best_score: best...
    return best_p, best_score
```
Need `t0` defined. Store `t0 = time.monotonic()` at start.
Potential bug with `last_recomp` initial: `iter_count - last_recomp >= recompute or last_recomp < 0`; last_recomp negative, condition true first. After weight recompute, set.
If after perturbation set last_recomp negative, next loop recompute.
If `use_fft` true, `lags/weights` may be old but not used.
If switching from direct to fft, What computed. Good.
If switching from fft to direct, What remains but not used.

The `F` variable is used for G; if use_fft false, no G FFT. Good.

We should maybe reset momentum? No.

Now, pair polish code. Need `_candidate_xs` helper? Use inline to avoid lambda.

```
def polish_pairs(f, n, N, deadline, rng, verbose=False):
    p=_normalize(f)
    c=_autocorr(p,N)
    best_score=2*n*c.max(); best_p=p.copy(); cur_score=best_score
    no_imp=0
    L=2*n-1
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        # current score may differ if p changed but c from p
        if len(c)==0: break
        # if current score much worse, revert
        cur_cmax = float(c.max()); cur_score=2*n*cur_cmax
        if cur_score > best_score * 1.03:
            p = best_p.copy(); c = _autocorr(p,N); cur_score=best_score; no_imp=0
        # top5 contrib
        top5 = np.argpartition(c, -5)[-5:]
        top5 = top5[np.argsort(c[top5])[::-1]]
        contrib = np.zeros(n)
        for k in top5:
            k=int(k)
            s=max(0, k-n+1); e=min(n-1,k)
            if s<=e:
                start=k-s; min_idx=k-e; stop=min_idx-1 if min_idx>0 else None
                contrib[s:e+1] += p[s:e+1] * p[start:stop:-1]
        # donor/receiver
        cmaxcopy = contrib.copy()
        i_d = int(np.argmax(cmaxcopy))
        cmincopy = contrib.copy()
        cmincopy[i_d] = -1e300
        j_r = int(np.argmin(cmincopy))
        if i_d == j_r:
            # choose another
            cmincopy2 = cmincopy.copy(); cmincopy2[j_r] = -1e300; j_r=argmin
        pairs = [(i_d,j_r)]
        if rng.random() < 0.25:
            i_rand = int(rng.integers(0,n)); j_rand=int(rng.integers(0,n))
            if i_rand != j_rand: pairs.append((i_rand,j_rand))
        improved = False
        for i,j in pairs:
            if i==j: continue
            m=p[i]+p[j]
            if m <= 1e-15: continue
            # Precompute A
            A = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
            A[i:i+n] += p
            A[j:j+n] -= p
            # candidate list
            candidates = [0.0, m, 0.1*m, 0.9*m, 0.5*m, p[i]+0.5*p[j], p[i]-0.5*p[j], 0.25*m, 0.75*m]
            # unique with tolerance and within
            seen=[]; cand=[]
            for x in candidates:
                if x < 0: x=1e-15
                if x > m: x=m-1e-15
                if abs(x - p[i]) < 1e-13 * max(1.0, m): continue
                if any(abs(x - y) < 1e-13*m for y in seen): continue
                cand.append(x); seen.append(x)
                if len(cand) >= 6: break
            best_x=None; best_sc=best_score
            # Also accept if improves current? We'll use best_score for global.
            for x in cand:
                dx = x - p[i]
                if abs(dx) < 1e-14: continue
                c_try = c.copy()
                c_try += (2.0 * dx) * A
                dx2 = dx * dx
                pos_ii = 2*i
                pos_jj = 2*j
                pos_ij = i+j
                if 0 <= pos_ii < L: c_try[pos_ii] += dx2
                if 0 <= pos_jj < L: c_try[pos_jj] += dx2
                if i != j and 0 <= pos_ij < L: c_try[pos_ij] -= 2.0*dx2
                # Due to numerical, some negative; max okay, but for next c use max with 0
                sc = 2.0*n*float(np.max(c_try))
                if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                    best_sc = sc; best_x = x; best_c = c_try
            if best_x is not None:
                p[i] = best_x
                p[j] = m - best_x
                p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
                s=p.sum()
                if s > 1e-300: p/=s
                c = np.maximum(best_c, 0.0)
                best_score=best_sc; best_p=p.copy(); no_imp=0; improved=True
                break
        if not improved:
            no_imp +=1
            if no_imp > 1000:
                # small perturb
                p = _perturb_logp(p, rng, 0.03, 1e-12)
                c = _autocorr(p,N)
                cur_cmax=float(c.max())
                if 2*n*cur_cmax < best_score:
                    best_score=...; best_p=p.copy()
                no_imp=0
    return best_p, best_score
```

```python
"""
Step-function optimizer minimizing  2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / (sum a)^2.

The main method is an annealed log-sum-exp mirror descent on the simplex.
Convolution coefficients are computed by FFT.  The gradient uses the exact
active-set gradient for a softmax weight vector over lags, either by direct
shifts for sparse weights or by a cached FFT for dense weights.  A sequence of
focused and exploratory variants is run from the supplied previous-best
sequence and from several chiral/random starts.  A final exact two-coordinate
polisher is applied.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math


def _next_pow2(m):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def _normalize(p, min_mass=1e-12):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    n = p.size
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    p = p / s
    if min_mass is not None and min_mass > 0:
        minp = min_mass / n
        p = np.maximum(p, minp)
        s = float(p.sum())
        if s > 1e-300:
            p = p / s
    return p


def _autocorr(p, N):
    L = 2 * p.size - 1
    F = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, N)[:L]
    return np.maximum(c, 0.0)


def _score_fast(p, N):
    n = p.size
    c = _autocorr(p, N)
    return 2.0 * float(n) * float(np.max(c))


def _start_key(item):
    return item[0]


def _add_start(starts, p, name, N):
    p = _normalize(p, 1e-12)
    sc = _score_fast(p, N)
    if np.isfinite(sc):
        starts.append((float(sc), name, p.copy()))
    return starts


def _shift_sum_direct(p, lags, weights, n):
    """G_i = sum_k weights[k] * p[k-i], valid indices only."""
    G = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    m = len(lags)
    for t in range(m):
        k = int(lags[t])
        w = float(weights[t])
        if w == 0.0:
            continue
        s = max(0, k - n + 1)
        e = min(n - 1, k)
        if s <= e:
            start = k - s
            min_idx = k - e
            stop = min_idx - 1 if min_idx > 0 else None
            G[s:e + 1] += w * p[start:stop:-1]
    return G


def _make_weights(c, temp):
    L = c.size
    if L == 0:
        return np.zeros(0), 0, 0.0
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    if temp <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(temp):
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return w, 1, cmax

    z = (c - cmax) / temp
    z = np.minimum(z, 0.0)
    z = np.maximum(z, -50.0)
    w = np.exp(z)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return w, 1, cmax

    w = w / s
    nz = int(np.count_nonzero(w > 1e-15))
    return w, nz, cmax


def _make_What(w, N):
    w_rev = np.ascontiguousarray(w[w.size - 1::-1])
    return np.fft.rfft(w_rev, N)


def _perturb_logp(p, rng, strength, min_mass=1e-12):
    n = p.size
    logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)
    st = float(strength)
    mode = int(rng.integers(0, 6))
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)

    if mode == 0:
        noise = rng.normal(size=n)
        noise -= float(noise.mean())
        logp += st * noise
    elif mode == 1:
        a = rng.normal(size=n)
        a -= float(a.mean())
        a = a - a[::-1]
        logp += 0.5 * st * a
    elif mode == 2:
        alpha = rng.uniform(-1.0, 1.0)
        logp += st * alpha * (t - 0.5)
    elif mode == 3:
        freq = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n // 4)))
        phase = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
        logp += 0.5 * st * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * freq * t + phase)
    elif mode == 4:
        freq = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n // 4)))
        phase = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
        s = np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * freq * t + phase)
        s = s - s[::-1]
        logp += 0.5 * st * s
    else:
        block = max(1, n // 32)
        nb = (n + block - 1) // block
        vals = rng.normal(size=nb)
        noise = np.repeat(vals, block)[:n]
        noise -= float(noise.mean())
        logp += 0.5 * st * noise

    logp -= float(logp.max())
    p2 = np.exp(logp)
    minp = min_mass / n
    p2 = np.maximum(p2, minp)
    s = float(p2.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return p.copy()
    return p2 / s


def optimize_lse(
    f0,
    n,
    N,
    deadline,
    rng,
    temp_ratio0=1.0,
    temp_ratio1=0.02,
    gamma0=1.0,
    gamma1=0.4,
    rho0=0.85,
    rho1=0.97,
    recompute=5,
    direct_threshold=64,
    verbose=False,
    label=""
):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    p = _normalize(f0, 1e-12)

    L = 2 * n - 1
    c = _autocorr(p, N)
    best_score = 2.0 * float(n) * float(np.max(c))
    best_p = p.copy()

    total_time = max(1.0, float(deadline - t0))
    if total_time <= 0.05:
        c = _autocorr(p, N)
        best_score = 2.0 * float(n) * float(np.max(c))
        return best_p, best_score

    cmax = best_score / (2.0 * float(n))
    w, nz, cmax = _make_weights(c, cmax * temp_ratio0)
    use_fft = nz > direct_threshold
    What = _make_What(w, N) if use_fft else None
    lags = np.where(w > 1e-15)[0]
    weights = w[lags]

    iter_count = 0
    last_recomp = -1000000000
    stall = 0
    patience = 3000
    minp = 1e-12 / n

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        iter_count += 1
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - t0
        prog = min(1.0, elapsed / total_time)

        if temp_ratio0 > 0.0:
            ratio = temp_ratio0 * (temp_ratio1 / temp_ratio0) ** prog
        else:
            ratio = temp_ratio1
        gamma = gamma0 + (gamma1 - gamma0) * prog
        rho = min(0.99, rho0 + (rho1 - rho0) * prog)

        F = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
        c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, N)[:L]
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
        cmax = float(np.max(c))
        score = 2.0 * float(n) * cmax

        if not np.isfinite(score):
            p = best_p.copy()
            c = _autocorr(p, N)
            score = best_score
            cmax = best_score / (2.0 * float(n))
            last_recomp = -1000000000
            continue

        if score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = score
            best_p = p.copy()
            stall = 0
            if verbose and (iter_count % 10000 == 0):
                print(f"    {label} iter {iter_count}: {score:.7f}")
        else:
            stall += 1

        if iter_count - last_recomp >= int(recompute) or last_recomp < 0:
            temp = cmax * ratio
            w, nz, cmax = _make_weights(c, temp)
            if nz > direct_threshold:
                What = _make_What(w, N)
                use_fft = True
            else:
                lags = np.where(w > 1e-15)[0]
                weights = w[lags]
                use_fft = False
            last_recomp = iter_count

        if use_fft:
            h = np.fft.irfft(F * What, N)
            G = np.asarray(h[L - 1:L - n - 1:-1], dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            G = _shift_sum_direct(p, lags, weights, n)

        if not np.all(np.isfinite(G)):
            p = best_p.copy()
            last_recomp = -1000000000
            continue

        Gspan = float(np.max(G) - np.min(G))
        if Gspan > 1e-30:
            eta = gamma / Gspan
            # max value is 0, range is gamma
            logfix = -eta * (G - np.min(G))
            fix = np.exp(logfix)
            fs = float(fix.sum())
            if fs > 1e-300:
                fix = fix / fs
                logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)
                logf = np.log(fix + 1e-300)
                lognew = rho * logp + (1.0 - rho) * logf
                lognew -= float(lognew.max())
                p = np.exp(lognew)
                p = np.maximum(p, minp)
                s = float(p.sum())
                if s > 1e-300:
                    p = p / s
                else:
                    p = best_p.copy()
        else:
            p = _perturb_logp(p, rng, 0.10, min_mass=1e-12)

        if stall > patience:
            if score > best_score * 1.02:
                p = best_p.copy()
            strength = 0.05 + 0.25 * (1.0 - prog)
            p = _perturb_logp(p, rng, strength, min_mass=1e-12)
            last_recomp = -1000000000
            stall = 0

    c = _autocorr(p, N)
    score = 2.0 * float(n) * float(np.max(c))
    if score < best_score:
        best_score = score
        best_p = p.copy()
    return best_p, best_score


def _run_lse(p0, n, N, task_deadline, rng, label, **params):
    if task_deadline - time.monotonic() < 5.0:
        p = _normalize(p0, 1e-12)
        sc = _score_fast(p, N)
        return p, sc
    return optimize_lse(
        p0, n, N, task_deadline, rng,
        verbose=True, label=label, **params
    )


def polish_pairs(f, n, N, deadline, rng, verbose=False):
    p = _normalize(f, 1e-12)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    c = _autocorr(p, N)
    best_score = 2.0 * float(n) * float(np.max(c))
    best_p = p.copy()
    no_imp = 0
    minp = 1e-12 / n

    if verbose:
        print(f"      polish start: {best_score:.7f}")

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        cur_cmax = float(np.max(c))
        cur_score = 2.0 * float(n) * cur_cmax

        if cur_score > best_score * 1.03:
            p = best_p.copy()
            c = _autocorr(p, N)
            cur_score = best_score
            no_imp = 0

        ksize = min(5, L)
        top = np.argpartition(c, -ksize)[-ksize:]
        top = top[np.argsort(c[top])[::-1]]

        contrib = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for kk in top:
            k = int(kk)
            s = max(0, k - n + 1)
            e = min(n - 1, k)
            if s <= e:
                start = k - s
                min_idx = k - e
                stop = min_idx - 1 if min_idx > 0 else None
                contrib[s:e + 1] += p[s:e + 1] * p[start:stop:-1]

        cmaxcopy = contrib.copy()
        i_d = int(np.argmax(cmaxcopy))
        cmincopy = contrib.copy()
        cmincopy[i_d] = -1e300
        j_r = int(np.argmin(cmincopy))

        pairs = [(i_d, j_r)]
        if rng.random() < 0.25:
            i_rand = int(rng.integers(0, n))
            j_rand = int(rng.integers(0, n))
            if i_rand != j_rand:
                pairs.append((i_rand, j_rand))

        improved = False
        for i, j in pairs:
            if i == j:
                continue
            m = p[i] + p[j]
            if m <= 1e-15:
                continue

            A = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
            A[i:i + n] += p
            A[j:j + n] -= p

            cand = [
                0.0,
                m,
                0.5 * m,
                0.2 * m,
                0.8 * m,
                p[i] + 0.5 * p[j],
                p[i] - 0.5 * p[j],
            ]
            cand_filt = []
            for x in cand:
                x = float(x)
                if x < 0.0:
                    x = 1e-15
                if x > m:
                    x = m - 1e-15
                if abs(x - p[i]) < 1e-13 * max(1.0, m):
                    continue
                ok = True
                for y in cand_filt:
                    if abs(x - y) < 1e-13 * max(1.0, m):
                        ok = False
                        break
                if ok:
                    cand_filt.append(x)
                    if len(cand_filt) >= 5:
                        break

            best_x = None
            best_sc = best_score
            best_c = None

            for x in cand_filt:
                dx = x - p[i]
                if abs(dx) < 1e-14:
                    continue
                c_try = c.copy()
                c_try += (2.0 * dx) * A
                dx2 = dx * dx
                ii = 2 * i
                jj = 2 * j
                ij = i + j
                if 0 <= ii < L:
                    c_try[ii] += dx2
                if 0 <= jj < L:
                    c_try[jj] += dx2
                if i != j and 0 <= ij < L:
                    c_try[ij] -= 2.0 * dx2
                sc = 2.0 * float(n) * float(np.max(c_try))
                if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                    best_sc = sc
                    best_x = x
                    best_c = c_try

            if best_x is not None and best_c is not None:
                p[i] = best_x
                p[j] = m - best_x
                p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
                p = np.maximum(p, minp)
                s = float(p.sum())
                if s > 1e-300:
                    p = p / s
                c2 = _autocorr(p, N)
                sc2 = 2.0 * float(n) * float(np.max(c2))
                if sc2 < best_score + 1e-13:
                    best_score = sc2
                    best_p = p.copy()
                    c = c2
                    no_imp = 0
                    improved = True
                    if verbose and improved and (no_imp == 0):
                        pass
                    break

        if not improved:
            no_imp += 1
            if no_imp > 1000:
                p = _perturb_logp(best_p, rng, 0.03, min_mass=1e-12)
                c = _autocorr(p, N)
                sc = 2.0 * float(n) * float(np.max(c))
                if sc < best_score:
                    best_score = sc
                    best_p = p.copy()
                no_imp = 0

    c = _autocorr(best_p, N)
    score = 2.0 * float(n) * float(np.max(c))
    if score < best_score:
        best_score = score
    return best_p, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(100.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = t_start + budget - 20.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    # Obtain previous best if available.
    try:
        h = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        h = np.where(np.isfinite(h), h, 0.0)
        h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if h.size < 1024:
            f_given = np.ones(4096, dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            f_given = h
            if f_given.sum() < 1e-12:
                f_given = np.ones(f_given.size, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        f_given = np.ones(6144, dtype=np.float64)

    n = int(f_given.size)
    # Keep FFT length modest.  For n <= 10922 this keeps N = 32768.
    max_n = 10922
    if n > max_n:
        f_given = f_given[:max_n]
        n = max_n

    f_given = _normalize(f_given, 1e-12)
    N = _next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    print(f"Length n={n}, FFT N={N}, budget={budget:.0f}s")

    starts = []
    _add_start(starts, f_given, "given", N)
    _add_start(starts, np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), "uniform", N)

    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)

    # Chiral random starts.
    for kk in range(10):
        logp = rng.normal(size=n)
        a = rng.normal(size=n)
        a -= float(a.mean())
        a = a - a[::-1]
        logp += 0.40 * a
        logp -= float(logp.max())
        p = np.exp(logp)
        _add_start(starts, p, f"chiral{kk}", N)

    # Tilts with chiral noise.
    for alpha, tag in [(0.5, "t05"), (1.0, "t10"), (1.5, "t15"), (2.0, "t20")]:
        p = np.exp(alpha * (t - 0.5))
        a = rng.normal(size=n)
        a = a - a[::-1]
        p = p * np.exp(0.30 * a)
        _add_start(starts, p, tag, N)

    # Low-dimensional Fourier-log starts.
    for kk in range(4):
        logp = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        modes = rng.integers(1, max(2, n // 4), size=6)
        for m in modes:
            m = int(m)
            amp = rng.uniform(0.3, 1.2)
            phase = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
            logp += amp * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * m * t + phase)
        logp -= float(logp.max())
        _add_start(starts, np.exp(logp), f"fourier{kk}", N)

    # Block-pulse random starts.
    for b, tag in [(8, "b08"), (16, "b16"), (32, "b32"), (64, "b64")]:
        nb = max(1, n // b)
        vals = rng.exponential(1.0, size=nb)
        p = np.tile(vals, (n + nb - 1) // nb)[:n]
        a = rng.normal(size=n)
        a = a - a[::-1]
        p = p * np.exp(0.20 * a)
        _add_start(starts, p, tag, N)

    if not starts:
        starts.append((2.0, "uniform_fallback", _normalize(np.ones(n), 1e-12)))

    starts.sort(key=_start_key)
    best_score = starts[0][0]
    best_f = starts[0][2].copy()

    print("Top initial starts:")
    for sc, name, _p in starts[:8]:
        print(f"  {name}: {sc:.7f}")

    # Main phase.  The sequence is split into fixed chunks; one task per chunk.
    available = deadline - t_start
    if available > 300.0:
        reserve_polish = max(120.0, 0.10 * available)
    else:
        reserve_polish = 0.0
    main_deadline = deadline - reserve_polish
    if main_deadline < t_start + 30.0:
        main_deadline = deadline - max(20.0, 0.05 * available)

    params_B = dict(
        temp_ratio0=0.40,
        temp_ratio1=0.008,
        gamma0=2.0,
        gamma1=0.6,
        rho0=0.80,
        rho1=0.95,
        recompute=3,
        direct_threshold=64
    )
    params_A = dict(
        temp_ratio0=1.00,
        temp_ratio1=0.020,
        gamma0=1.2,
        gamma1=0.4,
        rho0=0.85,
        rho1=0.97,
        recompute=5,
        direct_threshold=64
    )
    params_C = dict(
        temp_ratio0=0.80,
        temp_ratio1=0.005,
        gamma0=0.8,
        gamma1=0.2,
        rho0=0.90,
        rho1=0.99,
        recompute=8,
        direct_threshold=64
    )
    params_D = dict(
        temp_ratio0=1.50,
        temp_ratio1=0.003,
        gamma0=1.6,
        gamma1=0.8,
        rho0=0.82,
        rho1=0.94,
        recompute=3,
        direct_threshold=80
    )

    # (start_index, params, fraction_of_main_length, label)
    task_defs = [
        (0, params_B, 0.30, "B-best"),
        (0, params_A, 0.30, "A-best"),
        (1, params_A, 0.20, "A-start2"),
        (0, params_D, 0.10, "D-best"),
        (2, params_C, 0.10, "C-start3"),
    ]

    print("\nMain LSE optimization phase")
    main_len = max(0.0, main_deadline - t_start)
    cumulative = 0.0
    for si, par, frac, label in task_defs:
        cumulative += frac
        task_deadline = t_start + cumulative * main_len
        if task_deadline > main_deadline:
            task_deadline = main_deadline
        rem = task_deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 30.0:
            break

        if si == 0 or si >= len(starts):
            p0 = best_f
        else:
            sc_start, name_start, p_start = starts[si]
            if sc_start < min(1.95, best_score + 0.15):
                p0 = p_start
            else:
                p0 = best_f

        print(f"  Task {label}: start={float(sc_start if si and si < len(starts) else best_score):.7f}, alloc={rem:.0f}s")
        p_opt, sc_opt = _run_lse(p0, n, N, task_deadline, rng, label, **par)
        if np.isfinite(sc_opt) and sc_opt < best_score:
            best_score = float(sc_opt)
            best_f = p_opt.copy()
            print(f"  -> new best {best_score:.7f}")
        else:
            print(f"  -> best unchanged {best_score:.7f}")

    # Short refinement phase before polishing.
    if deadline - time.monotonic() > 120.0:
        extra_deadline = min(deadline - 30.0, time.monotonic() + 0.45 * (deadline - time.monotonic()))
        print("\nShort refinement")
        p_opt, sc_opt = _run_lse(best_f, n, N, extra_deadline, rng, "refine-C", **params_C)
        if np.isfinite(sc_opt) and sc_opt < best_score:
            best_score = float(sc_opt)
            best_f = p_opt.copy()
            print(f"  refinement new best {best_score:.7f}")
        else:
            print(f"  refinement best unchanged {best_score:.7f}")

    # Final two-coordinate polish.
    if deadline - time.monotonic() > 30.0:
        print(f"\nFinal polish, current best {best_score:.7f}")
        polish_deadline = deadline - 10.0
        p_pol, sc_pol = polish_pairs(best_f, n, N, polish_deadline, rng, verbose=True)
        if np.isfinite(sc_pol) and sc_pol < best_score:
            best_score = float(sc_pol)
            best_f = p_pol.copy()
            print(f"  polish new best {best_score:.7f}")
        else:
            print(f"  polish best unchanged {best_score:.7f}")

    best_f = np.asarray(best_f, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    best_f = np.clip(best_f, 0.0, 1000.0)
    final_list = [float(x) for x in best_f]

    try:
        official = evaluate_sequence(final_list)
    except Exception:
        official = best_score

    if np.isfinite(official):
        print(f"\nOfficial evaluation score: {official:.7f}")
    else:
        print("\nOfficial evaluation failed; using internal score.")
        official = best_score

    print(f"Final reported internal score: {best_score:.7f}")
    print(f"Time used: {time.monotonic() - t_start:.1f}s")

    return final_list
```
